# Statistical Methods: question papers in full

84 unique papers · 2006-07 to 2025-26 · Artificial Intelligence, Common (all branches), Chemical, Information Technology, Data Science, Civil, Electronics and Telecommunication, Mechanical, Computer Applications (MCA), AI and Data Science, Computer Science and Business Systems, AI and Machine Learning, Computer Engineering

Each paper: year, exam, semester, departments and programmes, then its full text as extracted. Scanned papers were read by OCR, so equations and symbols may contain recognition errors. Structured data: [`questions.jsonl`](questions.jsonl) (one record per question) and [`papers.jsonl`](papers.jsonl) (one record per paper). Index of files: [`README.md`](README.md).

• Premium Subscription: A binary variable indicating whether the user subscribed (1) or
did not subscribe (0).
The dataset is summarized below:   8
Premium Subscription (Class) Daily App Usage (hours) Avg. Monthly Spend ($)
1   4   120
1   5   140
1   3   110
0   1   50
0   2   60
0   1   55
Determine the LDA projection vector for this dataset.
********** 6 **********
````

SO:3   Let 𝑋1,, 𝑋2,,… 𝑋1𝑛 be a random sample from Poisson (θ)
BL:3
Then prove
T(𝑋𝑖)=∑𝑋𝑖,
is complete statistic
CO:3 c   6 M
SO:4   A salesman in a departmental store claims that at most 60 percent of the
BL:4   shoppers entering the store leave without making a purchase. A random
sample of 50 shoppers showed that 35 of them left without making a
purchase.
Are these sample results consistent with the claim of the salesman? Use an
LOS of 0.05.
********** 5 **********
````

Instructions: Candidates should read carefully the instructions printed on the question paper and on the cover
of the Answer Book, which is provided for their use.

1) Question No. _1__ is compulsory.

2) Out of remaining questions, attempt any 4 _ questions.

3) In all _5 questions to be attempted.

4) All questions carry equal marks.

5) Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.

6) Figures in brackets on the right hand side indicate full marks.

7) Assume Suitable data if necessary.

Q-1 A) According to Nielsen Media Research, approximately 67% of all U.S. households with television have cable TV. Seventy-
four percent of all U.S. households with television have two or more TV sets. Suppose 55% of all U.S. households with television
have cable TV and two or more TV sets. A U.S. household with television is randomly selected.

a. What is the probability that the household has cable TV or two or more TV sets? (2 marks)
b. What is the probability that the household has cable TV or two or more TV sets but not both? (2 marks)
c. What is the probability that the household has neither cable TV nor two or more TV sets? (3 marks)

Q-1 B) A study by. Peter D. Hart Research Associates for the Nasdaq Stock Market revealed that 43% of all American adults are
stockholders. In additidn, the study determined that 75% of all American adult stockho!ders have some college education. Suppose
37% of all American adults have some college education, An American adult is randomly selected.

a. What is the probability that the adult does not own stock? (1 mark)
b. What is the probability that the adult owns stock and has some college education? (1 mark)
c. What is the probability that the adult owns stock or has some college education? (1 mark)
d. What is the probability that the adult has neither some college education nor owns stock? (2 marks)
e. What is the probability that the adult does not own stock or has no college education? (2 marks)

Q-2 A) Tompkins Associates reports that the mean clear height for a Class A warehouse in the United States is 22 feet. Suppose
clear heights are normally distributed and that the standard deviation is 4 feet. A Class A warehouse in the United States is randomly

selected.

a. What is the probability that the clear height is greater than 17 feet? (2 marks)
b. What is the probability that the clear height is less than 13 feet? (2 marks)
c. What is the probability that the clear height is between 25 and 31 feet? (3 marks)

Vi
--- page 2 ---
Q-2 B) Market researcher Gartner Dataquest reports that Dell Computer controls 27% of the PC market in the United States.

Suppose a business researcher randomly selects 130 recent purchasers of PC.

a. What is the probability that more than 39 PC purchasers bought a Dell computer? (2 marks)
b. What is the probability that between 28 and 38 PC purchasers (inclusive) bought a Dell computer? (3 marks)
(2 marks)

c. What is the probability that fewer than 23 PC purchasers bought a Dell computer?

Q-3 A) According to Nielsen Media Research, the average number of hours of TV viewing per household per week in the United
States is 50.4 hours. Suppose the standard deviation is 11.8 hours and a random sample of 42 U.S. households is taken.

a. What is the probability that the sample average is more than 52 hours? (3 marks)
b. What is the probability that the sample average is less than 47.5 hours? (2 marks)
(2 marks)

c. What is the probability that the sample average is less than 40 hours?

Q-3 B) The 2000 U.S. Census asked every household to report information on each person living there. Suppose fora
sample of 30 households selected, the number of persons living in each was reported as follows.
. 2° 3 1 2 6 4 2 1 5 3 2 3 1 z 2
1 3 1 2 2 4 2 1 2. 8 3 2 1 1 3
Compute the mean, median, mode, range, lower and upper quartiles, and interquartile range for these data. (7 marks)

Q-4 A) According to a survey by Accountemps, 48% of executives believe that employees are most productive on Tuesdays.
Suppose 200 executives are randomly surveyed.

a. What is the probability that fewer than 90 of the executives believe employees are most productive on Tuesdays? (3 marks)
b. What is the probability that more than 100 of the executives believe employees are most productive on Tuesdays? (2 marks)
c. What is the probability that more than 80 of the executives believe employees are most productive on Tuesdays? (2 marks)

Q-4 B) Suppose the following data are selected randomly from a population of normally distributed values.
40 51 43 48 44 57 54 39 42 48 45 39 43

Construct a 95% confidence interval to estimate the population mean. (7 marks)

Q-5 A) In a manufacturing plant, machine A produces 10% of a certain product, machine B produces 40% of this product, and
machine C produces 50% of this product. Five percent of machine A products are defective, 12% of machine B products are
defective, and 8% of machine C products are defective. The company inspector has just sampled a product from this plant and has
found it to be defective. Determine the revised probabilities that the sampled product was produced by machine A, machine B, or

machine C. (7 marks)

-5 B) Suppose a random sample of 85 items has been taken from a population and 40 of the items contain the characteristic of
interest. Use this information to calculate a 90% confidence interval to estimate the proportion of the population that has the
characteristic of interest. Calculate a 95% confidence interval. Calculate a 99% confidence interval. (7 marks)

Q-6 A) Based on an indication that mean daily car rental rates may be higher for Boston than for Dallas, a survey of eight car rental
companies in Boston is taken and the sample mean car rental rate is $47, with a standard deviation of $3. Further, suppose a survey
of nine car rental companies in Dallas results in a sample mean of $44 and a standard deviation of $3. Use Level of Significance as
0.05 to test to determine whether the average daily car rental rates in Boston are significantly higher than those in Dallas. Assume
car rental rates are normally distributed and the population variances are equal. (7 marks)

Q-6 B) The average number of annual trips per family to amusement parks in the United States is Poisson distributed, with a
mean of 0.6 trips per year. What is the probability of randomly selecting an American family and finding the following?

a. The family did not make a trip to an amusement park last year. (Imark)
b. The family took exactly one trip to an amusement park last year. (1 mark)
c. The family took two or more trips to amusement parks last year. (2 marks)

Ne
--- page 3 ---
d. The family took three or fewer trips to amusement parks over a three-year period. (2 marks)
e. The family took exactly four trips to amusement parks during a six-year period. (1 mark)

Q-7 A) What is the first big change that American drivers made due to higher gas prices? According to an Access America
survey, 30% said that it was cutting recreational driving. However, 27% said that it was consolidating or reducing errands. If
these figures are true for all American drivers, and if 20 such drivers are randomly sampled and asked what is the first big change
they made due to higher gas prices,

a. What is the probability that exactly 8 said that it was consolidating or reducing errands? (2 marks)
b. What is the probability that none of them said that it was cutting recreational driving? (2 marks)
c. What is the probability that more than 7 said that it was cutting recreational driving? (3 marks)

Q-7B) According to the U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics, the average weekly earnings of a production worker in 1997 were $424.20.
Suppose a labor researcher wants to test to determine whether this figure is still accurate today. The researcher randomly selects 54
production workers from across the United States and obtains a representative earnings statement for one week from each. The
resulting sample average is $432.69. Assuming a population standard deviation of $33.90, and a 5% level of significance, determine
whether the mean weekly earnings of a production worker have changed. (7 marks)

raise had attended the training seminar? (7 Marks)

financial records of 25 of the Big Bazaar outlets. Big Bazaar claims that each outlet’s profits
have an approximately normal distribution with the same mean and a standard deviation of 40

million. If the Big Bazaar management is correct, then what is the probability that sample mean
for 25 outlets will fall within 30 million? (Hint: Z value =2.18) (12 Marks)

## 2012-13 · Final-Exam · Trim 6 · paper 1f93457f05ae

- Departments: Chemical, Mechanical
- Programmes: MBA TECH / CHEMICAL, MBA TECH / MECHANICAL
- Source: `02_Question_Papers/Legacy/MBA TECH/2ND YEAR/CHEMICAL/TRIM VI/SATISTICAL METHODES/STATISTICAL METHOD FINAL 2012-13.pdf` (+1 identical copies)
- Printed on paper: exam date: 11/5/2013
- Text: text (2 pages)

````text
SVKM's NMIMS
Mukesh Pate1 School of Technology Management &b Engineering
Programme: MBA (TECH) (ALL STREAMS) Year:   I1   Trimester: VI
I   Academic Year : 2012-2013
Subject:   Statistical Methods
Date:   11/5/2013
Final - Examination
Instructions: Candidates should read carefully the instruction
on the cover of the Answer Book, which is provided for their use.
Note:
1. Attempt all 5 questions.
2. Answer to each question to be started on a fresh page.
3. Figures in brackets on the right hand side indicate full marks.
Q. 1) Answer both the questions:
(i). An engineer tested nine samples of each of three designs of a certain bearing for a new electrical
winch. The following data are the no. of hours it took for each bearing to fail when the winch motor was
run continuously at maximum output, with a load on the winch equivalent to 1.9 times the intended
capacity. Calculate the mean and standard deviation for each group. Which design is best and why?
(5 marks)
Design
A   16   16   53   15   3 1   17   14   3 0   20
- B   18   27   23   2 1   22   26   39   17   28
- C   3 1   16   42   2 0   18   17   16   15   19
1
(ii). The administrator of a hospital surveyed the number of days 200 randomly chosen patients
stayed in the hospital f ~ l l o w i n ~ operation. a n   Answer the following questions; The data are:
Hospital   1-3   4-6   7-9   10-12   13-15   16-18   19-21   22-24
stay in days
Frequency   18   90   44   2 1   9   9   4   5
Calculate the standard deviation and mean According to Chebyshev's theorem, how
many stays should be between 0 and 17 days? How many are actually in that interval?
(5 marks)
Q.2) Attempt both the questions:
8
(i). In its third year, attendance in the Liberty Football League averaged 16,050 fans per game,
and had a standard deviation of 2,500. Accordingly what is the probability that the number of
fans at any given game was greater than 20,000?; fewer than 10,000?; between 14,000 and
17,500? [Given the different prob. values for z as 1.58 is 0.4429, 2.42 is 0.4922; 0.82 is 0.2939
and 0.58 is 0.21901   (5 marks)
--- page 2 ---
(ii). There are three factories F1, F2 and F3 that produce lo%, 30%, 60% of a certain item.
Suppose further that 1%, 2%, 3% of the items from factory F1, F2, F3 are respectively
defectives. One item is selected at random from the entire lot. What is the probability that it is
from factory F3 given that the item is defective? (5 marks)
Q.3) A multinational manufacturer uses a batch process to produce widgets. Each batch
of widgets takes 8 hours to produce and has material and labor costs of $8,476. Because of
variations in machine efficiency and raw material purity, the number of widgets per batch is
random. All widgets made can be sold for $2.50 each and widget production is profitable so
long as the batches sell for more than $12,500 on average. The manufacturer sampled 16 batches
and found 5,040 widgets per batch on average, with a standard deviation of 41.3 widgets. At a =
0.025, can the manufacturer conclude that its widget operation is profitable?
[Given t - 15,0.025 is 2.13 11   (1 0 marks)
Q.4) A supermarket manager is keeping track of the arrival of customers at check-out counters to
see how many cashiers are needed to handle the flow. In a sample of 500 five-minute time
periods, there were 22,74, 1 15,95, 94, 80 and 20 periods in which zero, one, two, three, four,
five or six or more customers respectively arrived at a check-out counter. Calculate the chi-
square statistic. Are these data consistent at the 0.05 level of significance with a Poisson
distribution with h = 3? [given chi-square distribution value with 6 degrees of freedom for 0.05 is
12.592; Poisson probabilities for h = 3 are for 0(.0498), l(. 1494), 2(.2240), 3(.2240), 4(. 1680),
5(. 1008), 16(.0840)]   (1 0 marks)
Q.5) Attempt both the questions:
(i). The following information is provided by a company to its subscribers to enable them to evaluate the
performance of mutual funds they are considering as potential investment vehicles. A recent survey
produced the following data on total annual rate of return over the past five years:   (6 marks).
Annual
return(%)   11-1 1.9; 12-12.9; 13-13.9; 14-14.9; 15-15.9; 16-16.9; 17-17.9; 18-18.9
Frequency   2;   2;   8;   10;   11;   8;   3;   1
1   a) Calculate the mean, variance and standard deviation of the annual rate of return for this
sample of 45 funds
b) According to Chebyshev's theorem between what values should at least 95 percent of the
sample observations fall? What percentage of the observations actually do'fall in that
interval?
c) Since the distribution is roughly bell shaped, between what values would you expect to find
68 percent of the observations? What percentage of the observations actually do fall in that
interval?
(ii). Hayes Textiles has shown the following percentage increase in net worth over the last 5 years; What
is the average percentage increase in net worth over the 5-year period?   (4 marks)
#   1992   1993   1994   1995   1996
5%   10.5%   9%   6%   7.5%
````

should the company conclude that there is an increased interest in the extra-spicy flavor?

Q.6) A supermarket manager is keeping track of the arrival of customers at check-out counters
to see how many cashiers are needed to handle the flow. In a sample of 500 five-minute
time periods, there were 22, 74, 115, 95, 94, 80 and 20 periods in which zero, one, two,
three, four, five or six or more customers respectively arrived at a checkout counter. Are
these data consistent at the 0.05 level of significance with a Poisson distribution
with X= 3? (10 marks).

Q.7) Answer both the questions:

(i). From a sample of 25 observations, the estimate of the standard deviation of the
population was found to be 15. From another sample of 14 observations, the estimate
was found to be 9.7. Can we accept the hypothesis that the two samples come from
populations with equal variances, or must we conclude that the variance of the second
population is smaller? Use the 0.01 level of significance? (5 marks)

(ii). Given a sample variance of 127 from a set of nine observations, construct a 95
percent confidence interval for the population variance. (5 Marks)

1s
--- page 3 ---
Q.8) Answer both the questions:
(i). Hinton Press hypothesizes that the average life of its largest web press is 14,500
hours. They know that the standard deviation of press life is 2,100 hours. From a sample
of 25 presses, the company finds a sample mean of 13,000 hours. At a 0.01 significance
level, should the company conclude that the average life of the presses is less than the
hypothesized 14,500 hours? (5 marks)

(ii). The university has decided to test three new kinds of lightbulbs. They have three
identical rooms to use in the experiment. Bulb 1 has an average life time of 1,470 hours
and a variance of 156. Bulb 2 has an average lifetime of 1400 hours and a variance of 81.
Bulb 3 has an average lifetime of 1,350 hours and a standard deviation of 6 hours. Rank
the bulbs in terms of relative variability. Which was the best bulb? (5 marks)

Q.9) Attempt both the questions
(i).Calculate Karl Pearson’s coefficient of correlation for the following data taking
deviations from actual mean 52 (X series) and 44 (Y series). (5 marks)

X series | 44 | 46 46 48 52 54 54 56 60 60
Y series | 36 | 40 42 40 42 44 46 48 50 52

(ii). There are three factories F1, F2 and F3 that produce 10%, 30%, 60% of a certain
item. Suppose further that 1%, 2%, 3% of the items from factory Fl, F2, F3 are
respectively defectives. One item is selected at random from the entire lot. What is the
probability that it is from factory F3 given that the item is defective? (5 marks)

Q.10) A study compared the effects of four 1-month point-of-purchase promotions on sales.
The unit sales for five stores using all four promotions in different months follow.

(10 Marks)
Free sample 78 87 81 89 85
One-pack gift 94 91 87 90 88
Cents off 73 78 69 83 76

Refund by mail 79 83 78 69 81
(a) Compute the mean unit sales for each promotion and then determine the grand mean.
(b) Estimate the population variance using the between-column variance
(c) Calculate the F ratio. At the 0.01 level of significance, do the promotions produce
different effects on sales?
